United Arab Emirates (2017). His Excellency Omar bin Sultan Al Olama: Minister of State for Artificial Intelligence. UAE Cabinet. Al Olama appointed October 2017 as the world’s first cabinet-level minister of state for artificial intelligence. url: https://uaecabinet.ae/en/fetch-cab-ministers/his-excellency-omar-bin-sultan-al-olama (visited on 08/25/2026).

United Nations (2024). Global Digital Compact. Annex to the Pact for the Future, adopted 22 September 2024 at the Summit of the Future. Adopted by all 193 UN member states; establishes an Independent International Scientific Panel on AI and a Global Dialogue on AI Governance. United Nations. url: https://www.un.org/en/summit-of-the-future/global-digital-compact (visited on 08/25/2026).

United Nations General Assembly (Dec. 1948). Universal Declaration of Human Rights. UNGA Resolution 217 A (III). Adopted in Paris, 48 in favor, none against, 8 abstentions (Byelorussian SSR, Czechoslovakia, Poland, Saudi Arabia, South Africa, Ukrainian SSR, USSR, Yugoslavia), 2 absent (Honduras, Yemen). url: https://www.un.org/en/about-us/universal-declaration-of-human-rights (visited on 08/25/2026).

— (2023). Resolution 78/241: Lethal Autonomous Weapons Systems. A/RES/78/241, adopted 22 December 2023. Adopted 22 December 2023 by 152 votes to 4 with 11 abstentions, on the report of the First Committee (A/78/409). A follow-on resolution, A/RES/79/62 of 2 December 2024, passed 166 to 3 with 15 abstentions. The Campaign to Stop Killer Robots, the advocacy coalition active on the issue, was founded in April 2013. United Nations. url: https://docs.un.org/en/a/res/78/241 (visited on 08/25/2026).

United States Congress (1965). Voting Rights Act of 1965, Section 4: Suspension of the Use of Tests or Devices in Determining Eligibility to Vote. Pub. L. No. 89-110, 79 Stat. 437; codified at 52 U.S.C. §10303. Cited for subsection (c), which defines a test or device as any requirement to demonstrate the ability to read, write, understand or interpret any matter, to demonstrate educational achievement or knowledge of a particular subject, to possess good moral character, or to prove one’s qualifications by the voucher of registered voters. Definition verified against the codified text. url: https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=(title:52+section:10303+edition:prelim) (visited on 09/06/2026).

United States District Court for the Northern District of California (2025). Bartz et al. v. Anthropic PBC, No. 3:24-cv-05417, Order on Motion for Summary Judgment on Fair Use (N.D. Cal. June 23, 2025). Order by Senior District Judge William Alsup. Held that training Claude on lawfully purchased/scanned books was “exceedingly transformative” fair use, while retaining pirated copies in a “central library” was not fair use; the piracy claims later settled for $1.5 billion, reported as the largest copyright recovery on record. url: https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69058235/bartz-v-anthropic-pbc/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

University of Chicago Data Science Institute (2026). Using AI and Data Science to Reliably Detect Internet Censorship in Real Time. University of Chicago Data Science Institute news. Describes a $1 million DARPA-funded project led by Prof. Nick Feamster (University of Chicago) with Princeton University and SRI International, using AI/statistical methods to distinguish deliberate censorship from ordinary network failure and produce a real-time map of state interference with internet traffic. url: https://datascience.uchicago.edu/news/using-ai-and-data-science-to-reliably-detect-internet-censorship-in-real-time/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

University of Helsinki (2020). Elements of AI Online Course to Expand Throughout the European Union. Elements of AI, created by the University of Helsinki and the Finnish company Reaktor, was translated into all official EU languages, an effort tied to Finland’s 2019 Presidency of the Council of the EU. url: https://www.helsinki.fi/en/news/teaching/elements-ai-online-course-expand-throughout-european-union (visited on 08/25/2026).

Vaswani, Ashish et al. (2017). “Attention Is All You Need”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 30 (NeurIPS 2017), pp. 5998–6008. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Vygotsky, Lev S. (1978). Mind in Society: The Development of Higher Psychological Processes. Ed. by Michael Cole et al. Cambridge, MA: Harvard University Press.

Waldron, Jeremy (2006). “The Core of the Case Against Judicial Review”. In: Yale Law Journal 115, pp. 1346–1406. Argues that judicial review is democratically illegitimate and that there is no reason to suppose rights are better protected by it than by a legislature. The case is explicitly conditional on a society having working democratic institutions and a citizenry that takes rights seriously. Cited as the standing objection an entrenched floor has to answer.

Watson, Gary (1975). “Free Agency”. In: The Journal of Philosophy 72.8, pp. 205–220. doi: 10.2307/2024703. Separates a valuational system from a motivational system, so that what an agent values and what an agent is moved by can come apart.

Wei, Jason et al. (2022). Chain-of-Thought Prompting Elicits Reasoning in Large Language Models. Prompting a model to produce intermediate reasoning steps sharply improves multi-step problem solving. arXiv: 2201.11903. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.11903 (visited on 08/28/2026).

WhatsApp (Jan. 2019). More Changes to Forwarding. Announces the worldwide extension, effective January 21, 2019, of the five-chat forwarding limit WhatsApp first imposed for users in India in July 2018, after viral rumors on the platform were tied to a string of mob killings. WhatsApp Blog. url: https://blog.whatsapp.com/more-changes-to-forwarding (visited on 08/25/2026).

Wisconsin Supreme Court (2016). State v. Loomis. 881 N.W.2d 749 (Wis. 2016), 2016 WI 68. Decided July 13, 2016; upheld consideration of a COMPAS risk-assessment score at sentencing over a due-process challenge, without requiring disclosure of the proprietary algorithm. url: https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=171690 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Wolf, Thomas et al. (2020). “Transformers: State-of-the-Art Natural Language Processing”. In: Proceedings of the 2020 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing: System Demonstrations. Association for Computational Linguistics, pp. 38–45. url: https://aclanthology.org/2020.emnlp-demos.6/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Zhao, Jieyu et al. (2017). “Men Also Like Shopping: Reducing Gender Bias Amplification using Corpus-level Constraints”. In: Proceedings of the 2017 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP). Association for Computational Linguistics, pp. 2979–2989. url: https://aclanthology.org/D17-1323/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Zhu, Ligeng, Zhijian Liu, and Song Han (2019). “Deep Leakage from Gradients”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 32 (NeurIPS 2019). Shows that private training data can be recovered from shared gradients — pixel-wise for images, token-wise for text — overturning the assumption that gradients are safe to share. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.08935 (visited on 08/29/2026).

Zhuang, Simon and Dylan Hadfield-Menell (2020). “Consequences of Misaligned AI”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 33. Gives necessary and sufficient conditions under which indefinitely optimizing an incomplete proxy objective — one with support on a subset of the attributes the principal values — drives overall utility arbitrarily low, in a resource-constrained world. Cited as the formal form of the aestheticized metric. arXiv: 2102.03896 [cs.AI]. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.03896 (visited on 09/01/2026).
